  19|                                                    Student Support Services
  20|             Given the exciting news that schools could return to 'normal' during the 2021-2022 school year, the Department of
  21|             Student Support Services focused its efforts on students' social and emotional wellness. Defining a new normal and
  22|             providing structured supports to students as they acclimated to the demands, pace and rigor of academics, vocational
  23|             programming and extracurricular activities was a top priority. Staff reflected on student data, strengthened community
  24|             partnerships, implemented new initiatives and expanded on the level of tiered supports offered to our student population.
  25|             The department continued its important mission to break the stigma of mental health, knowing that everyone adjusts
  26|             differently and handles stress in various ways. This unified approach, support, and collaboration helped our student body
  27|             display resilience, grow individually, and meet success.
  28|             At Monty Tech, we understand that everyone will require help at some point; that as individuals we all have strengths
  29|             and weaknesses, and as a result, we strive to implement thoughtful tiered services that promote a feeling of comfort for
  30|             students when accessing this support. In addition to the department's current school-based counseling services, Monty
  31|             Tech and LUK, Inc. collaborated throughout 2021-2022 with the Department of Public Health to provide services to at-
  32|             risk students and their families. The district also partnered with LUK to create a direct pipeline for telehealth referrals
  33|             and increased access to counseling supports. Programs such as the HOPE Squad; a peer to peer suicide prevention
  34|             program, Project AMP; a program to address prevention of substance use among youth ages 13-17, and Students Taking
  35|             A New Direction (STAND); a program to support students displaying at-risk behaviors have all been implemented.
  36|             The Department of Student Support Services has taken the necessary measures to ensure Social Emotional Learning
  37|             (SEL) is in the forefront of conversations when supporting students as they acclimate to returning to school full time
  38|             after lengthy periods of time away. Courses and activities designed to promote mindfulness, understanding, and social
  39|             emotional wellness are part of the many course offerings. Yoga and Meditative Art, Visual Arts, Directed Studies with
  40|             Social Emotional Learning lessons are all part of the efforts being implemented to ensure we address the needs of all
  41|             students and support their individual growth and development in a post pandemic world.
  42|             During the 2021-2022 school year, Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School District provided specialized
  43|             services to approximately three hundred students - measuring progress of over two hundred students on Individual
  44|             Education Plans (IEPs) and approximately one hundred students adhering to individualized Section 504 plans. While the
  45|             Student Support Services Department encompasses special education, the department provides support and is available
  46|             to all Monty Tech students.
  47|                                                                                              131

===PAGE 132=== (ocr, 46 lines)
   1|             The Student Support Services department includes a full-time nursing staff that continues to respond to COVID
   2|             concerns, administers medications, performs state-mandated health screening exams, and provides, when necessary,
   3|             health information to the special education team for a student's IEP meetings. The department benefits from a full-time
   4|             school social worker that participates in departmental meetings and assists students who have needs concerning finances,
   5|             family issues, homelessness, maternity, health issues, proper food and clothing, and military status deployments. The
   6|             school is also fortunate to have on staff a full-time psychologist, who evaluates all students referred for an initial
   7|             evaluation or who may require a three-year re-evaluation. In addition, we have a full-time speech language pathologist,
   8|             available to assist students with disabilities, assess these students and consult with teachers. Finally, our students also
   9|             have access to the services of a full-time adjustment counselor and part-time school psychologist. All of these
  10|             individuals are available for scheduled counseling sessions, mental health emergency treatment, medical/mental health re
  11|             -entry and transition support services, as well as crisis intervention.
  12|             The school's Director of Student Support Services oversees the District's Special Education Program, which is reviewed
  13|             annually in May, in accordance with regulatory requirements. The comprehensive review and evaluation are done in
  14|             collaboration with the Special Education Parent Advisory Council (SEPAC), and the results are used to improve the
  15|             special education procedures and programs in place at Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School. The district
  16|             will be working with the Department of Elementary and Secondary Education (DESE) during the 2022-2023 to review
  17|             all special education services, timelines, and programs.
  18|                                                     Technology @Monty Tech
  19|             The 2021-2022 school year was a welcome return to normalcy at Monty Tech, relieving the Technology Department of
  20|             the challenges presented by hybrid and remote instruction and learning that was necessary in previous years.
  21|             meant the department could focus its attention on vetting, selecting and beginning the migration to a new Student
  22|             Information System.              Technology specialists led this complex effort, moving away from Aspen X2 and into
  23|             Powerschool, which is expected to be a more user-friendly platform,                                       serving
  24|             students, parents and teachers more efficiently. In addition, the school implemented
  25|             Performance Matters, a software program that will track and analyze student
  26|             outcomes. By targeting student achievement and areas of concerns, teachers will be
  27|             able to immediately address challenges students are facing and offer remediation.
  28|             Across the school, students were provided with updated technology to improve
  29|             vocational training. The Business Technology program received new computers
  30|              with updated software for their students' use. Students and instructors in the
  31|             school's CAD/Drafting Technology program also received robust new computers
  32|             and associated software, and the school's busy Graphic Communications
  33|             department received long-awaited, high capacity copiers, so that their older
  34|             machines could be redistributed for teacher use throughout the campus.
  35|             Finally, the school invested in a Dell EMC Virtual server solution. This advanced
  36|             technology can handle the work of twenty-one physical servers, placing them into
  37|             four much more efficient nodes. These new nodes can handle up to seventy-five servers, so there is room for expansion
  38|             as the school's technology needs grow in future years.
  39|             Monty Tech students continue to benefit from a 1:1 Chromebook initiative, as the school is in its 6"h year of providing
  40|             every student with their own device. While the effects of the pandemic may have declined, lessons learned remain.
  41|             Instructors who found that communicating with students through Google Classroom or Schoology continue to post
  42|             lessons, collect assignments, and even offer assessments via this tool. Throughout the 2021-2022 school year, the
  43|             Technology Department continued to support the additional use of this technology, spending countless hours during the
  44|             school year upgrading the network and increasing the bandwidth coming into the school to sustain video and audio
  45|             conferencing, and increased network traffic.
  46|                                                                                              132

===PAGE 133=== (ocr, 41 lines)
   1|                                                Service Learning
   2|                    MARINE                      The Monty Tech Marine Corps Junior Reserve Officer Training Corps (MCJROTC) remains
   3|                                                one of the school's most worthwhile and engaging programs available to its students. This
   4|                                   CORPS        outstanding leadership program, based on the foundations of instilling a value of citizenship,
   5|                                                service to the United States, personal responsibility, and a sense of accomplishment, provides
   6|                                                students with countless opportunities to serve as ambassadors in their communities, through the
   7|                                                routine demonstration of student leadership development, volunteerism, perseverance, and
   8|                                                dedication.
   9|             During the 2021 - 2022 school year, MCJROTC Cadets participated in more than 2000 hours of community service and
  10|             volunteerism. Cadets assisted the Salvation Army by collecting more than $25,000 in donations, which provided aid to
  11|             countless families in need during the holiday season.
  12|             The Monty Tech MCJROTC cadets were actively involved in the Royalston Police Fair, providing assistance to local
  13|             vendors, the police department and community residents with the setup and
  14|             breakdown of this popular event. This annual event raised more than $4,500 to
  15|             support the Police Reform Act.
  16|             Our cadets also enjoyed service at the Wachusett Brewery-Rock & Roll 5K in
  17|             Westminster, MA, where they helped raise funds for the NEADS Organization.
  18|             NEADS trains service dogs for individuals with mental and physical disabilities,
  19|             many of whom are veterans.                 To ensure this program was a success, cadets
  20|             assisted with parking and traffic control for the runners, and assisted vendors with
  21|             setup and tear down.
  22|             Blacksmith Festival in Fitchburg MA. Monty Tech cadets assisted competitors
  23|             volunteerism and the craft of blacksmithing as a fine art.
  24|             Each year, we ask our school community to consider families who may not have a
  25|             bountiful, healthy meal to look forward to during the holidays. In 2021, we were
  26|             humbled by an outpouring of donations, which allowed our cadets to assemble
  27|             more than 100 Thanksgiving and Christmas baskets to be distributed to deserving families in the Monty Tech
  28|             Community.
  29|             While service to others and supporting community events are an essential part of our program, the Monty Tech cadets
  30|             report a special feeling of pride when assisting with local color guards and participating in community parades. In 2021-
  31|             2022, these special events were numerous, with as many as nine color guards local civic and veteran ceremonies, which
  32|             are always a highlight for students and instructors alike.
  33|             As our students learn the importance of giving back today, it is also important that they learn our history. To that end,
  34|             150 cadets travelled to Boston to walk the freedom trail, visit Faneuil Hall and learn more about our American history.
  35|             Finally, during the summer of 2022, 60 cadets participated in team building skills, leadership development and physical
  36|             fitness at Prince William Forest in Triangle, VA. They learned about the history of the Marine Corps as well as
  37|             American flight history by visiting the Marine Corps Museum, and the Dulles Air and Space Museum. Cadets also
  38|             travelled to the Marine Corps Base Quantico, where they received a class on Marine Corps and Foreign weapons from
  39|             the Marine Corps Weapons Training Battalion Staff. It wasn't all work, though. Our outstanding cadets enjoyed the
  40|             experience of water rafting, zip lining and a celebratory banquet to wrap the memorable week.
  41|                                                                                              133

===PAGE 134=== (ocr, 47 lines)
   1|                                          Evening Programs
   2|             The Monty Tech School of Continuing Education had an exciting FY22, receiving more than $600,000 in training
   3|             grants. School leaders applied for and received a generous Career Technical Initiative Grant, which provided the
   4|             funding needed to rollout new training programs, offered to qualified unemployed or underemployed participants at no
   5|             New training programs in Welding and HVAC were developed and saw their first cohort enroll. Students completed 250
   6|             hours of training, both in person and virtual, and left with industry-recognized credentials that include Hot Work
   7|             Certification, OSHA 10 Hour Certification, and an American Welding Society (AWS) Endorsement. Upon completion,
   8|             students were assisted, in partnership with MassHire North Central Career Center, in job placement.
   9|              As we continue to monitor workforce needs and skills gaps, the School of Continuing Education works closely with the
  10|             MassHire North Central Career Center to determine specific training programs that are most in-demand. Our
  11|             overarching goal to support the North Central Massachusetts workforce readiness pipeline with an increase in career and
  12|             licensure courses is evident in new programs that are developed, existing programs that are refined and in traditional
  13|             journeyman/apprenticeship programs that remain a hallmark of our institution.
  14|             Many other career-track students were eager to return in-person instruction. The popular Cosmetology program was able
  15|             to reopen their nighttime salon, providing services to clientele during the
  16|             evening hours, while electrical and plumbing programs were able to resume,
  17|             and students were back in the classroom, learning from experienced and
  18|             licensed professionals.
  19|             A large medical professional shortage arose in the workforce, due to the
  20|             pandemic. As a result, graduates from our evening medical programs continue
  21|             to be in high demand. Valuable training programs like Certified Phlebotomy
  22|             Technician, Certified Clinical Medical Assistant, Certified EKG Technician,
  23|             Certified Nurse Aide and Certified Home Health Aide, are able to help close
  24|             noted workforce shortages. With state recognized and nationally recognized
  25|             credentials, Monty Tech students are prepared and ready to join the regional
  26|             workforce.
  27|             Serving more than 1,000 community members annually, the Monty Tech
  28|             School of Continuing Education strives to provide an accessible and supportive
  29|             learning environment to an adult population seeking workforce training that
  30|             will lead to high-wage, high-skill jobs.
  31|             The Monty Tech Practical Nursing Program is designed to prepare graduates to practice safely and ethically in a caring
  32|             manner for patients who are experiencing common variations in health status in diverse health care settings.
  33|             On June 23, 2022 a graduating class of 16 students completed the Practical Nursing Program and entered the nursing
  34|             profession. The class has thus far achieved a pass rate of 95% on the NCLEX-PN exam (National Council Licensure
  35|             Examination for Practical Nurses). Program graduates are currently employed in the health care profession throughout
  36|             Massachusetts, working in various health care settings, such as long-term care, sub-acute care, mental health/substance
  37|             abuse facilities, physicians' offices and correctional medicine.
  38|             The Monty Tech Practical Nursing Program continues to strengthen the "LPN to BSN (Bachelor of Science in Nursing)
  39|             Bridge" relationship with Fitchburg State University (FSU). Former graduates have successfully transferred into seats in
  40|             the LPN to BSN Bridge program at FSU and will continue their education to a Bachelor's of Science in Nursing. Many
  41|             class applicants cited their attraction to Monty Tech as the opportunity to complete a ten-month accelerated program and
  42|             then bridge to Fitchburg State University. In the seven -year history of the Bridge Program, all of Monty Tech's Practical
  43|             Nursing graduates reaching graduation at FSU have passed their NCLEX-RN exam. These former graduates have gone
  44|             on to work in hospital settings including Heywood Hospital, Beth Israel, and Baystate Medical Center, while others have
  45|             pursued advanced degrees as Critical Care Nurse Practitioners, Nurse Educators, and Family Practice Nurse
  46|             Practitioners.
  47|                                                                                              134

===PAGE 135=== (ocr, 50 lines)
   1|                                                                   In 2021-2022, Monty Tech Practical Nursing students were asked to complete
   2|                                                                   patient scenarios in the Sim Labs in Terms 2 and 3. The Faculty Sim team has
   3|                                                                   implemented National League of Nursing (NLN) patient scenarios and authored
   4|                                                                   several others consistent with the National Council of State Board of Nursing
   5|                                                                   Detailed Test Plan. Instructors have also utilized the Sim lab setting to instruct
   6|                                                                   students in developing nursing skills that they may not be experiencing in the
   7|                                                                   clinical setting
   8|                                                                    By successfully developing and implementing a vast library of scenarios
   9|                                                                   reflecting International Association for Clinical Simulation and Learning
  10|                                                                   standards, we hope to prepare our students for any experience they may
  11|                                                                   encounter as a newly licensed Practice Nurse. In Maternity Sim Lab Boot Camp,
  12|                                     LPN                           for example, students utilize the simulation models to imitate the birthing
  13|                                                                   process,
  14|                                                                   complications. Well newborn care is also taught. Student feedback has been GETE
  15|             positive regarding these maternity scenarios, stating it better prepares them for an unexpected outcome during a delivery.
  16|             This expanded content also better prepares students for Nursing Acceleration Challenge Exam success at Fitchburg State
  17|             University.
  18|             Substance abuse and the mental health setting job opportunities have increased with more funding being provided to
  19|             combat the opiate crisis. In addition to having specialty clinical rotations, students participate in simulation scenarios
  20|             involving substance abuse and mental health clients, to help to prepare our graduates to work in a substance abuse
  21|             clinical setting.
  22|                                               Looking                                      Ahead
  23|             The 2022-2023 school year may bring new leadership to Monty Tech, but the
  24|             vision for improving programs and creative means to accomplish our collective
  25|             goals will remain intact. In any given year, school districts may make
  26|             improvements to facilities, upgrade instructional materials, or even implement
  27|             new and meaningful programs, but at Monty Tech, such evolution is expected. It
  28|             is imperative that each of our twenty-one vocational training programs stays
  29|             current, and that our training facilities are safe and reflect current industry
  30|             standards. Local budgets do not always allow for such improvements and
  31|             innovation, and so school officials continue to seek funding streams and
  32|             collaborative partnerships that allow the school to grow and expand to effectively
  33|             serve students in grades 9-12 and beyond. A few of the more notable initiatives
  34|             that we expect to have a positive impact on our school and students in the coming
  35|             years include:
  36|             Farm-to-table instruction and increased access to healthy foods: With a first-ever grant award from the Massachusetts
  37|             Executive Office of Energy and Environmental Affairs, Monty Tech looks forward to the establishment of an on-site
  38|             the recent COVID crisis, still felt by our students and their families today. With the capability of growing as many as
  39|             1,450 heads of lettuce every three weeks, this on-site farm will allow school officials to expand the school's already
  40|             generous meal program, saving the school as much as $38,000 in produce costs annually. School officials look forward
  41|             to using this incredible technology to infuse farm-to-table lessons into the Culinary Arts program, establish a Free Pantry
  42|             at the School for any student in need, and grant free produce to students and families in need.
  43|             Grant funding to improve and expand training programs: Monty Tech has applied for and received eight Skills Capital
  44|             Grants, totaling more than $2.5M. School officials anticipate a FY23 application may be forthcoming, and as a result,
  45|             have met to determine which program is in need of a shop renovation, updated equipment, and/or new technology that
  46|             reflects current industry standards. The school's Culinary Arts program, which operates a busy, full-service restaurant,
  47|             preparing students for both "front of the house" and "back of the house" careers may be the next vocational program to
  48|             benetit from Skills Capital grant funding, should an application be approved. School officials envision improvements to
  49|             the expansive training kitchen and restaurant area, to accommodate new and updated training equipment and industry
  50|                                                                                              135

  35|             ARTICLE 7. To see if the Town will vote to approve a reorganization of the Public Works Department in
  36|             accordance with a reorganization plan entitled "Administrative Organization Plan," dated April 5, 2022, and
  37|             accompanying message of the Town Manager, both on file at the office of the Town Clerk and Select Board
  38|             and posted on the town website, under the authority provided for in Article 5, Section 1(b) of the Town
  39|             Charter; or take any other action relative thereto. (Submitted by the Town Manager)
  40|             FINANCE COMMITTEE and SELECT BOARD recommend Approval. VOTED (Yes-136, No-28) to
  41|             approve the Administrative Organization Plan as presented by the Town Manager and as provided for
  42|             in Article 5, Section 1 (b) of the Town Charter:
  43|             It has been a mutually agreed upon goal of the Town Manager and Select Board since November 2020 to look at
  44|             the feasibility of creating an independent facilities department and improve the long term maintenance and
  45|             repair plan for town facilities. Currently the Town Manager delegates the responsibility of town facilities,
  46|             excluding School and Conservation properties, to the DPW Director. This Administrative Reorganization Plan
  47|             proposes to create a new Facilities Director position that will be responsible for all town facilities and grounds
  48|             maintenance.
  49|             The Town Manager, per the Town Charter Section 4-4 on the Town Manager's "Powers and Duties" Paragraph
  50|             (i), is "to have full jurisdiction over the rental and use of all town facilities and property except property under
  51|             the control of the school committee and the conservation commission; and shall be responsible for the
  52|             maintenance and repair of all town buildings and facilities placed under his control by this charter, by-law, vote
  53|             of the town or otherwise". This has been delegated to the DPW Director per the Town Charter Section 4-3
  54|             "Delegation of Power" that states: "The town manager may authorize any subordinate officer or employee to
  55|             exercise any power or perform any function or duty which is assigned to the office of town manager; provided,
  56|                           that all acts performed under such delegation shall at all times be deemed to be the acts of the Town
  57|                                                                                              142

===PAGE 143=== (ocr, 44 lines)
   1|             Currently the DPW Department encompasses Highway, Sewer, Cemetery, Parks, Facilities and oversight of the
   2|             Trash & Recycling Program. After reviewing the organizational structure of the Department of Public Works
   3|             and the limited staff within this department with a wide range and breadth of responsibilities, it is my
   4|             recommendation to create a new Facilities and Grounds Department headed by this new position.
   5|             Charter Process
   6|             The Administrative Plan process is outlined in the Town Charter, Article 5, as follows:
   7|             1. The Select Board shall hold one or more public hearings on the proposal giving notice by publication in a
   8|                  local newspaper, which notice shall describe the scope of the proposal and the time and place at which the
   9|                   hearing will be held, not less than 7 nor more than 14 days following said publication;
  10|             2. An organization or reorganization plan shall become effective at the expiration of 60 days following the date
  11|                  the proposal is submitted to the town meeting unless the town meeting shall, by a majority vote, within such
  12|                  period, vote to disapprove the plan. The town meeting may vote only to approve or to disapprove the plan and
  13|                   may not vote to amend or to alter it.
  14|             3. The Town Manager, through the administrative plan, and subject only to express prohibitions in a general
  15|                  law or this charter, may reorganize, consolidate or abolish any town agency, in whole or in part; establish
  16|                  such new town agencies as are deemed necessary and to the same extent as provided in section 5-1(a) for by-
  17|                  laws; and for such purpose may transfer the duties and powers and, so far as is consistent with the use for
  18|                   which the funds were voted by the town, transfer the appropriation of one town agency to another; provided,
  19|                  however, that no function assigned by this charter to a particular town agency may be discontinued or
  20|                  assigned to any other town agency unless specifically authorized by this charter.
  21|             4. Every reorganization plan submitted by the town manager pursuant to this section shall contain a proposed
  22|                   by-law which contains, in detail, such amendments, revisions, repeals or otherwise of existing ordinances as
  23|                   may be necessary to accomplish the desired reorganization.
  24|             5. Such reorganization plan and proposed ordinance shall be accompanied by a message from town manager
  25|                   which explains the benefits expected to ensue.
  26|             Adherence to Charter Process
  27|             1. A notice was published in the April 1, 2022 Lunenburg Ledger that a public hearing will be held at the April
  28|                  12, 2022 Select Board meeting to review the Administrative Organizational Plan presented by the Town
  29|                   Manager, in accordance with Section 5-1 (b) of the Town Charter, regarding the impending creation of the
  30|                  position of Facilities Director.
  31|             2. Relative to #3 above, there are no express prohibitions in the town bylaws or in the Town Charter that would
  32|                  not allow for the transfer of facilities and grounds duties from the DPW Director to a newly created Facilities
  33|                   Director position. The Charter does not assign the duties of facilities and grounds, exclusive of school and
  34|                   conservation, to another agency in town, nor are there any bylaws that relate to the oversight of facilities and
  35|                   grounds. The reorganization being sought is defined under Section 4-2, Powers and Duties of the Town
  36|                   Manager and is permissible under Section 4-3, Delegation of Authority, as outlined earlier.
  37|             3. The Town Manager, through the administrative plan, and subject only to express prohibitions in a general
  38|                   law or this charter, may reorganize, consolidate or abolish any town agency, in whole or in part; establish
  39|                   such new town agencies as are deemed necessary and to the same extent as provided in section 5-1(a) for by-
  40|                   laws; and for such purpose may transfer the duties and powers and, so far as is consistent with the use for
  41|                   which the funds were voted by the town, transfer the appropriation of one town agency to another; provided,
  42|                   however, that no function assigned by this charter to a particular town agency may be discontinued or
  43|                   assigned to any other town agency unless specifically authorized by this charter.
  44|                                                                                              143

===PAGE 144=== (ocr, 49 lines)
   1|             Message on the Benefits of Creating Director of Facilities and Grounds Position
   2|             The purpose of this reorganization plan is to create a Director of Facilities and Grounds position that would
   3|             oversee, supervise, and facilitate the comprehensive management of all town facilities and grounds. The reason
   4|             this is being recommended to town meeting, is that it will improve the long and short term management of our
   5|             facilities and grounds with an individual whose background and skills relate to property management, capital
   6|             planning, building construction, and grounds maintenance. This position will help the town strategize, prioritize
   7|             and coordinate maintenance, repairs, safety, and efficiencies of municipal properties, as well as play a key role in
   8|             future use and reuse of properties.
   9|             The key benefits of creating a Director of Facilities and Grounds Position:
  10|             1. Hiring a professional level position with a background in facilities management. Facilities management
  11|             experience is not typical for Department of Public Works Directors. An individual with this skill set will enhance
  12|             and improve the town's current and future facilities management plan.
  13|             2. A management level position whose sole focus is long term and short term planning and maintenance of town
  14|             facilities   and grounds. Since the DPW Director is responsible for multiple different divisions, town facilities
  15|             receive only a portion of that position's focus.
  16|             3. More preventative maintenance and less reactionary maintenance of our facilities and grounds.
  17|             4. Collaborate with the School's counterpart position on the overall maintenance plan for facilities and grounds
  18|             and work together with the Town Manager and Finance Director on capital planning and financing alternatives.
  19|             5. Directed effort towards the health and safety of all staff and users of town facilities.
  20|             6. 6. Create and maintain a property condition assessment of all town facilities and grounds utilizing Facilities
  21|                   Management software.
  22|             This position will be appointed by the Town Manager, in accordance with Sections 4-2 (d) of the Town Charter,
  23|             with ratification of the appointment to be made by the Select Board.
  24|             The Director of Facilities and Grounds will have direct supervision for the following existing positions: Facilities
  25|             Superintendent, Facilities Custodian, and Recreation Director. Those positions that are currently in the Laborers
  26|             International Union of North America, Public Employees, Local 39 will continue to assist with snow and ice
  27|             removal operations, mostly related to town facilities and grounds.
  28|             Essential functions of this position:
  29|             1. Responsible for the planning, organizing, directing and control of all aspects of Town facilities and grounds.
  30|                   This will include facility planning, renovation, construction, energy conservation measures and efficiencies,
  31|                   preventative and regular maintenance, and repair of all town-owned buildings and grounds
  32|             2. Responsible for a comprehensive approach to building maintenance and repair coordination, oversight,
  33|                  inspection, oversight of maintenance logs, resolving any warranty issues, establishing facility management
  34|                  schedules, and building security.
  35|             3. Formulates procedures related to Facilities and Grounds operations, develops and oversees employee
  36|                  schedules, and reviews and advises on daily maintenance issues.
  37|             4. Ensures all facilities staff attend routine training development for essential functions and the proper handling
  38|                  and application of all department equipment and tools; ensures staff obtain required licenses and safety
  39|             5. Focuses on the optimal facility use by staff, residents, and those who seek to conduct business with or in the
  40|                   town to ensure an effective user-experience.
  41|             6. Works with relevant town departments and elected Park Commission to advise and recommend long term
  42|                   plan for all town facilities and grounds. Updates the ten-year capital plan for all facilities and grounds
  43|                   projects that meet the Town's definition of a capital project and are not funded in the operating budget.
  44|                   Presents and answers all related questions for said plan and updates the Town Manager on the status of
  45|                   approved capital projects as they progress.
  46|             7. Collaborates with the School Facilities Director to ensure a town-wide approach to facilities management and
  47|                  sustainability for all properties. Work with the Town Manager and Finance Director to examine how large
  48|                  building projects fit into the town's overall debt schedule.
  49|                                                                                              144

===PAGE 145=== (ocr, 49 lines)
   1|             8. Acts as contract administrator for any town building or grounds project including the town facilities cleaning
   2|             contract and landscaping contracts.
   3|             9. Serves as the representative for any building or grounds project that requires permitting approval at a board/
   4|             committee/commission meeting (i.e. APDC, Historical, Planning Board, etc.).
   5|             10. Establishes and manages effective preventive maintenance schedules, building control system optimization
   6|             and analyties, and energy efficiency measures including warranty management of new and existing equipment
   7|             and appliances.
   8|             11. Ensures that all town building systems are serviceable, including ventilation, air conditioning, heating,
   9|             mechanical, electrical, refrigeration, monitoring and alarm systems.
  10|              12. Oversees the performance of various construction or repair work with contractors, vendors, suppliers and
  11|              Town employees; evaluates construction documents and plans associated with the maintenance and repair of
  12|             Town-owned buildings to ensure that projects are completed in accordance with specifications and within
  13|             established budgets.
  14|             13. Ensures that the procurement of price quotations and supplies related to the Facilities and Grounds
  15|             Department operations are in accordance with the State Procurement Law.
  16|             14. Responsible for coordinating with DPW director to ensure snow removal and winter safety procedures during
  17|             and after storms provide timely access to town facilities.
  18|             15. Provides information and/or advice related to any building construction, repair, reuse, or disposal of town
  19|             property.
  20|             16. Prepares and presents the operating budgets under this department; actively monitors utility and
  21|             maintenance expenses to identify needed efficiencies and to advise the Town Manager of any changes in spending
  22|             trends.
  23|             17. Engages in regular trainings and participates in trade shows and industry-events to remain current on
  24|             technological advancements and best practices.
  25|             The minimum qualifications that will be sought for this position include a Bachelor's Degree, preferably in
  26|             Construction Administration/Management, Business Administration, or Project Management. A qualified
  27|             candidate will possess at least 5 years of supervisory experience and 10 years of combined industry experience
  28|             related to construction, business management/energy management, and building system management. Essential
  29|             skills include superior verbal and written communications, policy formulation, preparing and providing public
  30|             presentations, and technological fluency in Microsoft Office and computerized maintenance management
  31|             systems. Successful candidates will have a working knowledge of automated temperature control systems, HVAC,
  32|             carpentry, electrical, plumbing and general building systems, and Energy Management Systems (EMS) and
  33|             controls; or any equivalent combination of education, training and experience which provides the required
  34|             knowledge, skills and abilities to perform the essential functions of the job. Must be knowledgeable about the
  35|             International Building Code and OSHA requirements. Previous municipal employment is not required. The FY
  36|             2023 budgeted amount for this position is $85,000, plus benefits.

===PAGE 153=== (ocr, 48 lines)
   1|                                       5/7/22 ATM Article 22: Salary Administration Plan Bylaw Amendments
   2|             § 70-1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE
   3|             This by-law establishes a Salary Administration Plan, hereinafter called the "Plan", which classifies several
   4|             positions of town services into groups, establishes salary schedules, establishes so called "fringe" benefits, or
   5|             "indirect pay" provisions, such as holidays and vacations with pay, sick leave, and the like, and establishes a
   6|             Personnel Committee to administer the Plan, as hereinafter provided, and to represent the interest of both
   7|             taxpayers and town employees.
   8|             § 70-2. PERSONNEL COMMITTEE
   9|                     A. There is hereby established a Personnel Committee consisting of five members, each residing in
  10|                          the Town of Lunenburg, to be appointed by the SelectmenSelect Board, and each member shall
  11|                           serve for a term of three years; provided, however, that of the members originally appointed, one
  12|                           shall serve a term of one year, two for a term of two years, and two for a term of three years, as
  13|                           designated by the-SelectmenSelect Board. However, nothing herein shall be deemed to prohibit
  14|                          the chairman of the Personnel Committee from designating a member of said committee to serve
  15|                          as a representative of said Committee on a study committee of the Town Meeting, nor shall such
  16|                          service affect the tenure of said member of the Personnel Committee. If any member of the
  17|                           Personnel Committee shall resign or otherwise vacate the office before the expiration of their
  18|                          term, the successor shall be appointed by the SelectmenSelect Board, at their discretion, to serve
  19|                          for the balance of the unexpired term.
  20|                     B. A Town employee may have a staff assignment to serve as a subject matter expert and non-voting
  21|                           member of the Personnel Committee and may be called upon from time to time to provide
  22|                          information requested by the Personnel Committee so that the Personnel Committee can make
  23|                           well informed decisions and proposals.
  24|                     C. Said Committee shall elect its own chairman and vice-chair by September July 15* of each yearat
  25|                           their first meeting after June 30t of each year; and in the absence or incapacity of the chairman, the vice- chair shall
  26|                                                             act as chairman. The Committee shall keep a record of its official
  27|                           proceedings and actions, may establish its own rule of procedure and may, subject to
  28|                           appropriation therefore, retain                  such clerical and other assistance, and make such other
  29|                           expenditures as may be necessary to the performance of its duties. Three members shall constitute
  30|                          a quorum for the transaction of the business of the committee, and the affirmative vote of three
  31|                           members shall be necessary to any official act of the Committee.
  32|                     D. The Personnel Committee is hereby authorized to administer the provisions of this by-law, except
  33|                          for such duties as may be specifically assigned by statute, Town Charter or by-law to other Town
  34|                           officers, boards or committees. Appeals for decisions of the Personnel Committee may be taken
  35|                          to the Town at the next Annual or Special Town Meeting.
  36|                     E. The Personnel Committee may from time to time issue, amend, or revoke Administrative Orders
  37|                           for the purpose of giving effect to the provisions of this by-law, and for the purpose of giving
  38|                           effect to the provisions of such other by-laws and votes of the Town, pertaining to personnel
  39|                           administration, as said Committee may be responsible for administering and/or enforcing. Each
  40|                           such order shall be numbered in sequence, and said Committee shall maintain in the Town Office
  41|                           a file for all such orders issued, said file to be open to public inspection.
  42|                     F. Except as otherwise provided by law, the Committee shall have access to all facts, figures, records
  43|                           and other information relating to the personnel of Town departments, and the same shall be
  44|                           furnished forthwith by any such department whenever so requested by the Personnel Committee,
  45|                          in such a form as said Committee may require.
  46|                     G. In the event that the Personnel Committee does not have enough members to conduct business, the
  47|                           Town Manager shall have the authority to administer the Salary Administration Plan.
  48|                                                                                              153

===PAGE 154=== (ocr, 39 lines)
   1|             § 70-3. CLASSIFICATION AND SALARY SCHEDULE
   2|             The positions of all employees in the service of the Town, and not otherwise covered by a collective
   3|             bargaining agreement or employees of the school, are hereby classified by titles in the groups listed in
   4|             Schedule A, entitled "Classification and Salary Schedule", as set forth in this by-law.
   5|             § 70-4. TITLE OF POSITIONS
   6|             No person shall be appointed, employed or paid as an employee in any position subject to the provision of the
   7|             Salary Administration Plan under any title other than those of the Classification and Salary Schedule, or under
   8|             any title other than that of the job the duties of which are actually performed. The job title in the Classification
   9|             and Salary Schedule of the Plan shall be the official title for all purposes having to do with the position and
  10|             shall be used to designate the position in all payrolls, budget estimates and official reports, and in every other
  11|             connection involving personnel and fiscal processes.
  12|             § 70-5. NEW OR CHANGED POSITIONS
  13|             The Personnel Committee tentatively may add new job title and descriptions to this Plan, establishing grades
  14|             with minimum and maximum wage or salary brackets for the same, and reclassifying existing jobs, revising
  15|             the description and the minimum and maximum wage and salary brackets therefore, and such addition or
  16|             reclassification to be effective only for the period from the date of such action by the Personnel Committee to
  17|             the date of any vote with respect thereto adopted at a subsequent Town Meeting but in no case later than the
  18|             next Annual Town Meeting unless such later date as may be specified by such vote. Any increase in wages or
  19|             salaries under this section shall be effective only if funds are available for the purpose, and approved by the
  20|             Finance Committee and Beard of Selectmen Select Board.
  21|             § 70-6. RECLASSIPICATION OF EMPLOYEES
  22|             No employee shall be reclassified to a job in another group, either higher or lower, until the Personnel
  23|             Committee shall have determined that such a reclassification will be consistent with the provisions of the
  24|             Salary Administration Plan. Eligibility for a step increase of a reclassified employee will be granted one year
  25|             from the date of reclassification.
  26|             § 70-7. JOB DESCRIPTIONS AND INTERPRETATIONS
  27|             A. The Personnel Committee shall maintain written job descriptions of the jobs or positions in the Plan,
  28|             describing the essential functions, requirements and duties. The descriptions shall not be interpreted as either
  29|             complete or restrictive and employees shall continue to perform any duties assigned by an employee's
  30|             supervisor.
  31|             B. Every three (3) years, the Personnel committee shall review the wage and salary schedules of all positions
  32|             in the Plan, and be in a position to recommend to the Board of Selectmen Select Board any action deemed
  33|             desirable to maintain a fair and equitable pay level.
  34|             § 70-8. SALARY SCHEDULE
  35|              A. The Classification and Salary Schedule of the Salary Administration Plan, set forth in Schedule A of this by
  36|             -law, shall consist of the maximum and minimum salaries and step-rate salaries for the groups and positions in
  37|             the Classification and Salary Schedule. The salary range of a group shall be the salary range for all positions
  38|             classified in such group.
  39|                                                                                              154

===PAGE 155=== (ocr, 39 lines)
   1|                     B. Irrespective of the period of time for which pay rates may be shown in the Salary Schedule, all
   2|                           employees shall continue to be paid on an hourly, weekly, monthly, or other basis, as at present,
   3|                           unless otherwise authorized by the Personnel Committee or by amendment of the Salary
   4|                           Administration Plan.
   5|             §70-9. SALARY RATES ABOVE MAXIMUM
   6|             Any salary rate whichrate that is above the maximum rate for a job, as established by Salary Administration
   7|             Plan, shall be deemed to be a personal rate, and apply only to the incumbent. When such incumbent leaves the
   8|             employ of the Town or is transferred to another job with a higher maximum rate than their personal rate or
   9|             higher maximum rates are established, the personal rate shall disappear. No other employee assigned to or
  10|             hired for such job shall advance beyond the maximum of the job.
  11|             §70-10. LAYOFF PROCEDURE; RECALL PROCEDURE
  12|             A. Layoff Procedure - If the Town decides to layoff employees, it shall determine from which classification
  13|             (s) or job function(s) layoffs shall be made. Layoffs shall then be made based on seniority within the
  14|             classification.
  15|                  1) "Seniority" shall be the length of an employee's continuous service measured from the employee's most
  16|             recent appointment date; provided an employee's seniority may be reduced for periods of unpaid leave, unless
  17|             otherwise prohibited by law.
  18|                 2) If two or more employees in the same classification have the same seniority, the employee who has the
  19|             shorter term of continuous employment with the Town shall be laid off. If there are two or more employees in
  20|             the same classification with the same seniority and the same term of continuous employment with the Town, a
  21|             part-time employee shall be laid off before a full-time employee. If there are still two or more employees in the
  22|             same classification, the Town Manager shall determine which employee shall be laid off.
  23|               3)An employee that is scheduled to be laid off shall have the right to bump other employees in lower
  24|             classifications with less seniority, provided that the Town Manager, in his/hertheir sole discretion, but with
  25|             consultation of the Department Head, shall make a determination that the employee retained is qualified and
  26|             able to perform the duties of the lower classification, subject to disability discrimination laws.
  27|                4) Employees who have been laid off from employment are responsible for keeping the Town advised of
  28|             their current address for the purpose of potential recall from layoff.
  29|             B. Recall Policy - If the town conducts a recall from layoff, recall shall be offered in reverse order of layoff by
  30|             classification provided the laid off employee is qualified and able to perform the duties of the classification to
  31|             which the employee is being offered recall, subject to disability discrimination laws. Employees shall only be
  32|             eligible for recall from layoff under the terms of this policy for one (1) year from the date of the employee's
  33|             layoff.
  34|             1) Any employee who is recalled from layoff under the terms of this policy shall retain his/hertheir seniority,
  35|             less the period of layoff.
  36|             2) The Town will notify an employee of a recall opportunity by certified letter (or other verifiable method of
  37|             delivery) to the employee's last known address. An employee who has been offered an opportunity to be
  38|             recalled from layoff must advise the Town of his/hertheir intention to accept recall within five (5) working
  39|                                                                                              155

===PAGE 156=== (ocr, 39 lines)
   1|             § 70-11. TRANSFERS AND PROMOTIONS
   2|             A. When an employee is promoted to a job with a higher rate range, or rate of pay, he/she shall enter it at the
   3|             minimum of the job rate range or at their own rate, whichever is the higher, but in no event at a rate in excess
   4|             of the maximum for such job. Upon promotion, the employee will be placed on the pay grade for the new
   5|             position at the lowest step at which the employee would receive a pay increase. They may also receive at the
   6|             time one or more step rate increases, provided the maximum for the job is not exceeded, if the Department
   7|             Head recommends that qualification and performance warrant it, and the Personnel Committee approves. If the
   8|              Department Head should feel that there should be a trial period before recommending the promotional step rate
   9|             increase or increases, on recommendation of the Department Head, the Personnel Committee may approve any
  10|             such deferred promotional increase at the conclusion of the trial period.
  11|             B. fan employee is transferred to a job with a lower rate range, or rate of pay, they shall enter it at the pay
  12|             rate closest to their current ratetheir own rate or at the maximum rate for the job, whichever is the lower.
  13|             § 70-12. NEW PERSONNEL
  14|             A. The hiring rate shall be the minimum of the rate range of the job for which the new employee if is hired,
  15|             unless otherwise authorized by the Personnel Committee. the Town Manager recommends paying the
  16|             employee at a higher step due to the employee's qualifications and experience or marketing conditions. The
  17|             Personnel Committee must vote to approve any recommendation above step 1. Department Heads shall notify
  18|             the Town Manager of the hiring of all new personnel. This notification shall be done in writing via the New
  19|             Hire Personnel Form.
  20|             B. Effective July 1, 2015, for new regular full-time and new regular part-time personnel for whose position
  21|             January andre, eligibility for a stop inorcase will dupond on ic comploye date ortire If hired boreen ", the employee is not eligible for a step increase until July 1" of the following calendar
  22|             year. If hired between July 1s and December 31", the employee is eligible for their first step increase on the
  23|             upcoming July 1st
  24|             C. Effective July 1, 2022, for new regular full-time and new regular part-time personnel, eligibility for a step
  25|             increase will be on the anniversary date of employment.
  26|             § 70-13. GRIEVANCE PROCEDURE
  27|             A. Any employee who believes that they have been received inequitable treatmentsubject to a violation of the
  28|             Salary Administration Plan is encouraged to discuss the grievance initially with their supervisor within 10
  29|             working days of the occurrence or knowledge of the event. If the employee is not comfortable discussing the
  30|             matter with their direct supervisor, or if the matter is not settled, the employee should submit the said
  31|             grievance to their Department Head. The Department Head shall meet with the employee and will respond in
  32|             writing to the employee within 14 days of the meeting.
  33|             B. If the grievance is not resolved by the decision of the Department Head, the employee may escalate the
  34|             grievance to the Assistant Town Manager/HR Director. If unsatisfied, the employee may file an appeal in
  35|             writing with the Town Manager within 10 days of receipt of the most recent decision. The Town Manager will
  36|             meet with the employee within a reasonable time after receipt of the appeal and will respond in writing to the
  37|             employee's grievance within 30 days after meeting with the employee. The Town Manager's decision shall be
  38|             final and binding.

===PAGE 157=== (ocr, 41 lines)
   1|             § 70-14. DEPARTMENT BUDGETS
   2|             Each Department Head shall include in the annual budget request, a pay adjustment section to provide funds
   3|             for anticipated pay adjustments during the ensuing year, expenditures to be made therefrom only in accordance
   4|             with the Salary Administration Plan with approval of the Personnel Committee.
   5|             § 70-15. SEVERABILITY
   6|             In the event any provision of this by-law or application thereof, shall be held to be invalid by the proper
   7|             authorities, this shall not be construed to affect the validity of any other provision, or application thereof, of
   8|             this by-law.
   9|             § 70-16. CIVIL SERVICE
  10|             Nothing in this by-law of Salary Administration Plan shall be construed to conflict with Chapter 31 (Civil
  11|             Service) of the General Laws.
  12|             § 70-17. HOURS OF WORK; OVERTIME; COMPENSATORY TIME
  13|             A. Non-Exempt Employees:
  14|             The schedule of hours to be worked shall be determined by the appropriate Department Head.
  15|                                1) For non-exempt positions entitled to overtime pay at one and one-half (1 1/2) times the
  16|                                      regular rate for work actually performed in excess of forty (40) hours within a work
  17|                                      weekworkweek,. the The words "work actually performed" shall be construed to include
  18|                                      all duly paid time off, including but not limited to holidays, sick leave, vacation, personal
  19|                                      days and bereavement leave, and such leave shall be included in the computation of forty
  20|                                      (40) hours.
  21|                                2) Unauthorized leave or leave for which the employee is not entitled to compensation shall
  22|                                      not be included in such computation, unless otherwise required by law.
  23|               B. Hours by occupation.
  24|               1. Industrial Trades and Service Occupations
  25|                           a.. Work Week 40 hours in 5 days.
  26|                           b. One and one-half (1 1/2) times the regular rate will be paid for work actually performed in
  27|                            excess of forty (40) hours within a work week.
  28|               2. Office Occupations
  29|                           a. Work Week - Forty (40) hours in five days and may be reduced administratively to 36 hours in
  30|                              four or five days.
  31|                           b. One and one-half (1 1/2) times the regular rate will be paid for work actually performed in
  32|                            excess of forty (40) hours within a work week.
  33|               3. Public Safety Occupations
  34|                           Fire Department - The hours of the Fire Department shall be determined by the Chief with the
  35|             -approval of the Personnel Committee provided, however, that all hours worked by any member
  36|             -of the Fire Department will be paid for at the regular rate then in force.
  37|             B. Exempt Employees:
  38|                   Exempt Employees, Administrative and Professional Occupations, are not governed by hours of the
  39|                  positions under their supervision or control. Generally, such positions shall be for at least forty (40)
  40|                  hours per week when full-time and not entitled to overtime.
  41|                                                                                              157

   2|                           Persons employed in these positions are not governed by hours of the positions under their
   3|                                     supervision or control. Generally, such positions shall be for at least forty (40) hours per week
   4|                                     when full-time and not entitled to overtime.
   5|                           C. Notwithstanding anything in the foregoing paragraphs, the appropriate department head or his/
   6|                           her designee may, instead of paying the overtime rate, provide that the employee take
   7|                           compensatory time off at a rate of 1 1/2 times the hours worked that quality for overtime pay.
   8|                           Compensatory time must be taken within 60 days.

  33|                                                                       After completion of 20+ Years of continuous service 200
  34|                           B. Earned vacation                                                                                                 time up to and
  35|                          including 80 hours must be used in the year granted. No unused portion of this allowance shall be
  36|                           carried forward into a subsequent vacation year, nor shall any monetary compensation be given
  37|                           for such unused portion. Earned vacation time in excess of 80 hours should be used in the year
  38|                           granted, and no portion of such excess shall be carried forward into a subsequent year. If
  39|                           departmental scheduling or other serious extenuating circumstances preclude the use of all such
  40|                           excess in the year granted, the employee may request to receive compensation equivalent to such
  41|                           unused portion in a lump sum payable on the first payroll warrant in July, in addition to their
  42|                           regular salary, or may be allowed to carry over such excess as the Town may elect in its sole
  43|                           discretion. Any employee asserting a serious extenuating circumstance must submit a written
  44|                           request to his/hertheir direct supervisor and such request must be approved by the direct
  45|                           supervisor and the Town Manager before the employee shall be compensated for, or allowed to
  46|                           carry over, such excess.
  47|                           C. Employment Termination. An employee whose employment ends, whether voluntary
  48|                          resignation, retirement or involuntary termination, shall receive be paid for all accrued, but
  49|                           unused vacation

   1|                    leave.compensation equivalent to earned vacation allowance, provided he/she has been in the
   2|                           continuous service of the Town for 10 months, as follows:
   3|               1. All currently available, unused vacation allowance up to a maximum of 200 hours; and
   4|               2. One twelfth (1/12) of the vacation allowance which would otherwise have been credited on the next
   5|               succeeding July 1, for each full month worked since the preceding July 1.
   6|             D. The vacation year shall begin July 1. In the first year of employment, employees shall accrue one
   7|             (1) day of vacation leave (prorated based on number of hours worked each week) per month of service
   8|             completed to a maximum of 80 hours to be available for use by the employee on the following July 1st
   9|             Vacation leave accrued each fiscal year will be available for use on the following July 1s. Any change in the
  10|             rate of accrual shall occur on the employee's anniversary date. When an employee's anniversary date for the
  11|             completion of the 5', 10"-20" years occurs after July 1st                           ' the employee shall receive vacation leave for the
  12|             remainder of the year on a pro-rated basis.

  14|             designee for such time or times as best serve the public interest., provided, however, that each employee shall
  15|             be entitled to use at least 40 hours of their vacation accrual during the period between June 1 and September
  16|             15 if desired, based on seniority.
