  20|             approval of the by the Town Manager. In no case shall any such advanced vacation exceed the number of days
  21|             for which the employee would be entitled to compensation, were they to terminate their services in good
  22|             standing on the first day of such vacation.
  23|             § 70-19. PAID HOLIDAYS
  24|             A. The Town of Lunenburg shall observe the following holidays:
  25|                    New Year's Day                                                             Labor Day
  26|                     Martin Luther King Day                                                   Columbus Day
  27|                     Presidents Day                                                           Veterans' Day
  28|                     Patriots Day                                                             Thanksgiving Day
  29|                     Memorial Day                                                             One Floating Holiday*
  30|                     Juneteenth                                                               Day Before Christmas
  31|                     Independence Day                                                         Christmas Day
  32|               B. Employees who regularly work forty (40) hours per week, and/or five (5) days per week shall receive
  33|               paid holiday leave on the day on the Town observes the holiday based on the base hourly rate of the
  34|               employee for the number of hours the employee is regularly scheduled to work on the day of the week
  35|               on which the holiday is observed.
  36|             B. C. Employees who regularly work less than forty (40) hours and/or five (5) days per week shall receive paid
  37|             holiday leave on the day the Town observes the holiday provided the employee is regularly scheduled to work
  38|             on the day the Town observes the holiday, based on the hourly rate of the employee for the number of hours
  39|             the employee is regularly scheduled to work on the day of the week on which the holiday is observed.
  40|             C. D. Holiday leave will be granted on the day the Town observes the holiday, when possible. If the holiday
  41|             falls on a Saturday, it will be observed on the preceding Friday. It the holiday falls on a Sunday, it will be
  42|             observed on the following Monday. If an eligible employee is required to work on the day the Town observes
  43|             the holiday, they will be entitled to a Floating Day, which has to be used within 30 days, by agreement of the
  44|             supervisor and employee. Employees shall receive a floating holiday* for any holiday that falls on a day that
  45|             they are not regularly scheduled to work. If the department head determines that holiday leave cannot
  46|                                                                                              159

===PAGE 160=== (ocr, 44 lines)
   1|                 granted, the employee shall receive payment at one and one-half times the base hourly rate for all hours worked
   2|                 on the holiday, in addition to his or her holiday pay. Final determination is to be made by the Town Manager.
   3|                 B. E. To be eligible for paid holiday leave, an employee must work his or her full assigned shift on his or her
   4|                 regularly scheduled workday before a holiday, and the next regularly scheduled working day following the
   5|                 holiday, unless the employee has been excused for a legitimate reason by the direct supervisor.
   6|                 * Floating Holiday time for employees who work less than 40 hours per week will be calculated in hours and
   7|                 determined by dividing the number of hours regularly worked per week, by the number of days they work in one
   8|                 week.
   9|                 § 70-20. SICK LEAVE
  10|                            A. All persons employed, full-time or part-time who have completed the six (6) months probationary
  11|                            waiting period by July 1 of any year, shall be entitled to sick leave of ninety-six (96) hours, to be credited
  12|                            on July 1. The part time compensation Vacation and Sick Leave formula shall be used to determine the
  13|                            employee's prorated sick pay (See SECTION 28. DEFINITIONSSection 28).
  14|                            B. New employees having completed the six (6) months probationarywaiting period shall be credited as
  15|                            of the end of said six (6) months, with sick leave in the amount of eight (8) hours for each full month
  16|                            intervening between the six months anniversary date and the next July 1, up to the maximum of ninety-
  17|                            six (96) hours. Use of such leave must be occasioned by sickness or injury of the employee or their
  18|                            immediate family member.
  19|                            C. Unused sick leave shall be cumulative up to a maximum of 1,200 hours, and shall be carried forward
  20|                            on July 1 of each year, to be added to the sick leave credited as of July 1. The vacation and sick leave
  21|                            formula shall be used to determine the employee's prorated maximum sick hours (See SECTION 28.
  22|                            DEFINITIONS). Sick leave may be donated to another employee who has exhausted their accumulated
  23|                            sick leave at the discretion of the Town Manager.
  24|                            D. When an employee finds it necessary to be absent because of accident or illness, they shall report the
  25|                            fact to their immediate supervisor as soon as possible either in person or by agent. Sick leave will not be
  26|                            granted unless such report is made. For the protection of the Town, the Department Head or supervisor
  27|                            may require the presentation of a doctor's certificate in connection with a claim for sick leave that
  28|                            exceeds, or is equal to 24 hours (or three missed days of work)., and may, if it is deemed advisable, send
  29|                            a doctor or nurse to investigate any absence alleged to be caused by illness. Any employee who refuses
  30|                            to submit to a doctor's examination note shall not be entitled to sick leave.
  31|                            E. An employee who retires in good standing shall receive compensation based on a percentage of
  32|                            his/hertheir accumulated unused sick leave, up to a maximum of 480 hours, as follows: 5 years continuous
  33|                            service 10%; 15 years continuous service 50%; 25 years continuous service 75%. In the event of an
  34|                            employee's death, the above benefit will be paid to his/hertheir beneficiary. This retirement benefit will
  35|                            not be available to any employee hired after June 30, 2010.
  36|                            F. Sick Time can be used for any of the following reasons:
  37|                                  1) Care for the employee's own physical or mental illness, injury or medical condition that requires
  38|                                       home care or rest, professional medical diagnosis or care, or preventative medical care;
  39|                                 2) Care for the employee's child, spouse, parent or parent of a spouse, who is suffering from a
  40|                                       physical or mental illness, injury, or medical condition that requires home care, professional
  41|                                       medical diagnosis or care, or preventive medical care;
  42|                                 3) Attending the employee's routine medical appointment or a routine medical appointment for the
  43|                                       employee's child, spouse, parent, or parent of spouse.
  44|                                                                                              160

===PAGE 161=== (ocr, 39 lines)
   1|                § 70-21. PERSONAL LEAVE
   2|                           A. All persons employed, full time or part time who have completed six (6) months probationary waiting
   3|                           period by July 1 of any year, shall be entitled to personal leave of three (3) days twenty four (24) hours,
   4|                           to become available on July 1 for use during the ensuing fiscal year. The part time compensation formula
   5|                           shall be used to determine the employee's prorated personal hours (See Section 26).
   6|                           B. New employees having completed the six (6) months probationarywaiting period shall be credited as
   7|                           of the end of said six (6) months, with eight one (81) personal hours day for use during the period between
   8|                           said six (6) months anniversary and the end of the then current fiscal year; provided, however, that the
   9|                           said six (6) months probationarywaiting period must have been completed by May 1 of that year.
  10|                           C. Employees who do not use any sick leave within a three month (3) month period (calculated
  11|                           quarterly), will be entitled to eight one (81) additional personal hours day for each such quarter; said
  12|                           personal hours days are to be used in the same fiscal year in which they are earned. Personal leave shall
  13|                           be scheduled with the approval of the Department Head and/or his/hertheir designee. Personal leave shall
  14|                           not be cumulative.
  15|                § 70-22. FAMILY AND MEDICAL LEAVE
  16|                A. The Town agrees to abide by the provisions of the 1993 Family and Medical Leave Act (FMLA). All eligible
  17|                employees are entitled to up to twelve (12) weeks of unpaid leave for certain family and medical reasons in a one
  18|                (1) year period. Employees are eligible for FMLA if they have worked for the Town for at least one (1) year and
  19|                at least for one thousand two hundred and fifty (1,250) hours over the previous twelve (12) months.
  20|                B. Unpaid EMLA leave must be granted for any of the following reasons:
  21|                1. To care for the employee's child after birth, or placement for adoption or foster care
  22|                2. To care for the employee's spouse, son, daughter, or parent, who has a serious health condition
  23|                3. For a serious health condition that makes the employee unable to perform the employee's job
  24|                C. In accordance with the Town's policy, the Town requires the use of paid leave time during the FMLA absence
  25|                including accumulated Sick Leave, Vacation Leave, and Personal Leave. The Town requires all employees
  26|                applying for an FMLA leave to notify their Department Head, in writing, thirty (30) days prior to the leave, if
  27|                possible provides leave in accordance with the Family and Medical Leave Act (FMLA) and its FMLA Policy,
  28|                as adopted by the Select Board and as it may be amended from time-to-time. A copy of the Town's FMLA Policy
  29|               is available on the Town's website.
  30|                § 70-23. BEREAVEMENT LEAVE
  31|                In the event of death in an employee's family (wife, husband, mother (in-law), father (in law), child, grandparents,
  32|                brother, or sister) up to and including three days off duty may be granted, with pay, not to be charged against sick
  33|               leave. (spouse, children, parents, in-laws, step-children, step-parents, significant other or someone who is under
  34|               your direct care and living in your home, up to and including five (5) days off duty shall be granted, with pay. In
  35|                the event of death of a grandparent, grandchild, sibling, sibling-in law, step-sibling, uncle, aunt, nephew, niece,
  36|               three (3) days off duty shall be granted, with pay. Two (2) additional days off, with pay, may be granted at the
  37|               discretion of the Town Manager not to be charged against sick leave. Any time above these days is to be charged
  38|                against sick leave.
  39|                                                                                              161

===PAGE 162=== (ocr, 39 lines)
   1|               § 70-24. MILITARY LEAVE
   2|               The Town provides military leave in accordance with state and federal law.
   3|                A. Town employees with a minimum of six consecutive month's employment with the Town who serve in the State
   4|               or Federal military training forces under orders shall be allowed full pay for not more than one normal working week
   5|               and shall be granted a military leave of absence for the period of their required service with said forces.
   6|                В. Military leave of absence with pay shall be granted to employees of the Town on occasion of their appearance
   7|               before local draft boards or draft appeal boards, or for physical examinations ordered by said boards; but no such leave
   8|               shall be granted for a period of more than one day without the approval of the Personnel Committee. Such leave shall
   9|               be deemed to be a military leave of absence.
  10|                C. A military leave of absence without pay shall be granted to any Town Officer or employee called to active duty
  11|               with the State or Federal Armed Forces for purposes other than the routine annual tour of duty for training purposes.
  12|                D. As used herein, the phrases "military training forces" or "armed forces" shall mean the United States Air Force, the
  13|                United States Army, the United States Navy, the Unites States Marine Corps, the United States Coast Guard, the
  14|               Massachusetts National Guard, the Massachusetts Air National Guard, the Massachusetts State Guard, and any and all
  15|                components thereof.
  16|                § 70-25. JURY DUTY
  17|                All persons permanently employed, whether full-time or part-time, for a minimum of twenty (20) hours per week,
  18|                called for jury duty shall be paid by the Town, an amount equal to the difference between the compensation paid for a
  19|                normal working period and the amount paid by the court, excluding allowance for travel, and this will be certified by
  20|               the Town Clerk or Treasurer upon presentation of the check for monies received for Jury Duty. Jury duty pay is for up
  21|               to three days of pay.
  22|                § 70-26. LONGEVITY PAY
  23|                After reaching ten (10) years of employment, and thereafter, each employee permanently employed, whether full or
  24|               part time, shall receive an annual bonus equal to 2% of their annual salary, and is payable in the first pay period after
  25|                the employee's anniversary date, at 15 years of continuous service - 2.5% of their annual salary, and at 20 years of
  26|                continuous service - 3% of their annual salary.
  27|               § 70-27. TRAVEL REIMBURSEMENT
  28|                Travel reimbursement shall be by signed voucher at a rate established by the IRS when using the employee's own car,
  29|               plus tolls and parking fees.
  30|               § 70-28. DISCIPLINE POLICY
  31|                A. Items which constitute grounds for disciplinary action against an employee include: chronic absenteeism or
  32|               tardiness, insubordination or refusal to work, infraction of work and safety rules, threat of physical violence against
  33|               fellow employees, use of profane language to a supervisor or fellow employee, or offenses of comparable gravity or
  34|               severity.
  35|                B. Process.
  36|                      1) Upon determination by the Department Head that disciplinary action is warranted, the following guidelines
  37|                           shall be implemented for all employees that have successfully completed a six (6) month probationary waiting
  38|                           period:
  39|                                                                                              162

===PAGE 163=== (ocr, 44 lines)
   1|                 STEP 1.                1st Offense - Written warning.
   2|                 STEP 2.                2nd Offense - A formal written reprimand notifying the employee of a one -day suspension and
   3|                                       that one further offense shall result in a three-day suspension without pay.
   4|                 STEP 3.                3rd Offense - Employee will receive an immediate three -day suspension without pay and a
   5|                                        written notification that any further offense shall be grounds for dismissal.
   6|                 STEP 4.                4th Offense - Immediate dismissal of the employee.
   7|                       1) Depending on the particular circumstances, one or more of the above steps may be skipped. The offense
   8|                            for which disciplinary action is taken may be any combination of the items described above. For example,
   9|                            offense number one could be tardiness, offense number two could be absenteeism, and offense number
  10|                            three could be refusal to work or insubordination. If an employee goes twenty-four
  11|                            (24) months without a further offense, the first offense beyond twenty-four (24) months shall be
  12|                            disciplined at the level of step one.
  13|                       2) The following infractions are grounds for the immediate dismissal of an employee for just cause by the
  14|                            Department Head. Just cause may include, but shall not be limited to, the following:
  15|                 1) Willful neglect or non-performance of one or more assigned duties;
  16|                 2) Demonstrated incompetence in the performance of one or more assigned duties;
  17|                 3) Behavior that seriously interferes with the normal operation of the municipality, the department, or any
  18|                       member of the workforce:
  19|                 4) Insubordination, which shall mean a refusal to carry out a direct order;
  20|                 5) Dishonesty in the performance of assigned duties:
  21|                 6) Chronic absenteeism or tardiness without reasonable excuse;
  22|                  7) Unauthorized possession or use of alcohol or an unprescribed controlled substance during any period of
  23|                       assigned work;
  24|                 8) Municipal theft.
  25|                 SECTION 28. DEFINITIONS
  26|                 The following terms used in the Salary Administration Plan shall be defined as set forth herein:
  27|                 Full-Time Employee shall mean any person employee who spends an amount of time betweenis regularly
  28|                 scheduled to work thirty-six (36) and forty (40) hours of permanently scheduled work per week in the service of
  29|                 the Townhours or more per week.
  30|                 Hourly Employee shall mean any employee of the Town who by virtue of their classified position is compensated
  31|                 at an hourly rate for services rendered.
  32|                 Intermittent Employee shall mean any person employee who works less than tweis regularly scheduled to work
  33|                 fewer than twenty (20) hours per week-on a regular basis. Intermittent employees are not eligible for benefits (i.e.
  34|                 vacation, sick-time, holiday, longevity, insurance, unless required by lawete.) and work less than one thousand
  35|                 forty (1,040) hours per year in the service of the Town.
  36|                 Part Time Compensation Vacation and Sick Leave Formula shall be determined by dividing the compensation of
  37|                 time off by 40 and then multiplying by the number of hours the employee works per week. For example, a 40
  38|                 hour per week employee is eligible for 120 hours of vacation. If another employee with the same tenure works 36
  39|                 hours, the formula is as follows: 120 / 40 = 3. Then multiply 3 x 36 hours = 108 hours.
  40|                 Part-Time Employee shall mean any person employee who spends-is regularly scheduled to work at least twenty
  41|                 (20) hours and fewerless than thirty-six (36) hours but twenty (20) hours or more of permanently scheduled work
  42|                 per week in the service of the Town.
  43|                 Probationary Waiting Period shall mean the initial six (6) month period of employment with the Town.
  44|                                                                                              163

===PAGE 164=== (ocr, 43 lines)
   1|               Seasonal Employee shall mean any employee, part or full-time who spends works no more than six (6)
   2|               months180 days-or one hundred eighty (180) days whichever is less per year., in the service of the Town.
   3|               Seasonal employees are not eligible for benefits (i.e. vacation, sick-time, holiday, longevity, insurance,
   4|               etc. unless required by law).

  24|             be posted on the Commission's Town website.

  28|             of this by-law; provided, however, that neither the Commission's failure to promulgate a rule or regulation nor
  29|             a legal declaration of the invalidity thereof shall act to suspend or invalidate the effect of the provisions hereof.
  30|             Amendments) to the rules and regulations may be contemplated during regular, open meetings of the

  32|             hearing published in a local newspaper at least five (5) business days in advance. At the discretion of the
  33|             Commission, the hearing may be continued from time to time as necessary. Following adoption, the

  38|             Except as otherwise stated herein, the Sewer Commission reserves the right to waive, in its sole discretion, any

  41|                                                                                              166

===PAGE 167=== (ocr, 45 lines)
   1|             (1) emergency circumstances threaten the safe and continued use of a property that a waiver may resolve;
   2|             (2) alternatives to the waiver present an undue hardship to any person or entity affected thereby;
   3|             (3) the Commission deems it to be in the best interest of the Town of Lunenburg and not otherwise
   4|             inconsistent with the purpose and objectives hereof;
   5|             Notwithstanding the above, no waiver shall be granted for a new commercial property.
   6|             Each request for a waiver shall be made in writing to the Commission, shall require a public hearing to be
   7|             conducted with notice of said hearing published in a local newspaper at least five (5) business days in advance
   8|             and, shall require an affirmative vote of approval by a super-majority in number of the Commission. In no
   9|             event shall the Commission waive any requirement of the Massachusetts General Laws or any regulation
  10|             adopted by the Commonwealth thereunder. There shall be no entitlement to a waiver; and the grant of a
  11|             waiver in one instance shall not require the Commission to grant a waiver in any other,"

   6|             ARTICLE 30. To see if the Town will vote to adopt the following "Resolution in Support of Changing the
   7|             State Flag & Seal of Massachusetts":
   8|             Whereas the history of Massachusetts is replete with instances of conflict between the European Colonists and
   9|             the Native Nations of the region, who first extended the hand of friendship to the Colonists on their shores in
  10|             1620, and helped them to survive starvation during the settlers first winters on their land;
  11|             Whereas members of the Native Nation for who the State of Massachusetts is named were ambushed and
  12|             killed by Myles Standish, first commander of the Plymouth Colony, in April of 1623, barely two years after
  13|             the Pilgrims arrived on their shores;
  14|             Whereas the Colonial broadsword brandished above the head of the Native man on the Massachusetts State
  15|             Flag and Seal is modeled after Myles Standish's own broadsword, borrowed for that purpose from the Pilgrim
  16|             Hall in Plymouth by the illustrator Edmund Garrett in 1884;
  17|             Whereas the belt binding the Native's cloak on the Flag and Seal is modeled after a belt worn by Metacomet,
  18|             known to the English as King Philip, who was among the Wampanoag leaders who resorted to a mutually
  19|             destructive war in 1675-76 in defense of Native lands against Colonial encroachment;
  20|             Whereas the proportions of the body of the Native man in the Flag and Seal were modeled from a Native
  21|             skeleton kept in Winthrop, the bow modeled after a bow taken from a Native man shot and killed by a colonist
  22|             in Sudbury in 1665, and his facial features taken from a photograph of an Ojibwe chief from Great Falls,
  23|             Montana, considered by the illustrator to be a "fine specimen of an Indian," though not from Massachusetts;
  24|             Whereas the history of relations between Massachusetts since Colonial times and the Native Nations who
  25|             continue to live within its borders includes the forced internment of thousands of so-called "praying Indians"
  26|             on Deer Island, in Boston Harbor, where they died by the hundreds of exposure in 1675, their subsequent
  27|             enslavement in Boston, Bermuda, and the Caribbean Islands, the offering of 40 pounds sterling as bounty for
  28|             the scalps of Native men, women and children in Massachusetts beginning in 1686, increased to 100 pounds
  29|             sterling for the scalps of Native adult males by 1722, half that amount for Native women and children;
  30|             Whereas Native Americans were legally prohibited from even stepping foot into Boston from 1675 until 2004,
  31|             when that law was finally repealed;
  32|             Whereas the 400 anniversary of the landing of the colonists at Plymouth Plantation in the year 2020, afforded
  33|             every citizen of the Commonwealth a chance to reflect upon this history and come to an awareness of the
  34|             possibility of a better relationship between the descendants of the Colonial immigrants and the Native Nations
  35|             of the Commonwealth;
  36|             Whereas Native Americans have long suffered the many abuses of racism, the appropriation of their symbols
  37|             for public schools and sports teams, the loss of their ancestral lands and the encroachment of their cultural
  38|            lifeways;
  39|             Whereas the area now known as the Town of Lunenburg shares a rich Native history with modern day tribal
  40|             Nations, who inhabited this area long before the first colonial settlers arrived circa 1718, such as the Abenaki
  41|             and the Nipmuc; Now, therefore, BE IT RESOLVED that the Town of Lunenburg hereby adopts this
  42|             resolution in support of the work of the Special Commission Relative to the Seal and Motto of the
  43|             Commonwealth, established by a Resolve of the General Court in 2020 and appointed by the Governor to
  44|                                                                                              168

===PAGE 169=== (ocr, 49 lines)
   1|             recommend changes to the current flag and seal of Massachusetts; and in support of a new seal and motto for
   2|             the Commonwealth that may better reflect our aspirations for harmonious and respectful relations between all
   3|             people who now call Massachusetts home. The town clerk shall forward a copy of this resolution to Sen. Marc
   4|             Pacheco and Rep. Antonio Cabral, co-chairs of the Joint Committee on State Administration, who serve as
   5|             members of the Special Commission Relative to the Seal and Motto of the Commonwealth, and to Sen. John
   6|             Cronin, Rep. Michael Kushmerek and Rep. Danillo Sena, with the request they support the work of the
   7|             aforementioned Special Commission. (Submitted by Citizen Petition) FINANCE COMMITTEE
   8|             RECOMMENDATION: No Direct Financial Impact, SELECT BOARD recommend Approval. VOTED (Yes-
   9|             48, No-20) to adopt "Resolution in Support of Changing the State Flag & Seal of Massachusetts".

   1|                                    C.         Upon receipt of the Application the APDC shall review and hold a
   2|                                               published hearing on the application. Said hearing to be noticed by an ad
   3|                                               published in a newspaper with local circulation 14 days or more before the
   4|                                              hearing. Within 45 days of the application submittal, a copy of which shall
   5|                                              be filed with the Town Clerk, the APDC shall hold a public hearing on the
   6|                                              application, said hearing to be noticed by an ad published at least once in a
   7|                                              newspaper with local circulation 14 days or more before the hearing.
   8|                                               Following the hearing, the APDC shall determine whether or not the
   9|                                               proposed alteration is consistent with the standards set forth in § 113-9 and
  10|                                              the associated design guidelines. If the APDC determines by majority vote
  11|                                              that the alteration adheres to the standards and the design guidelines, it
  12|                                               shall issue a certificate to alter, which may be subject to reasonable
  13|                                               conditions that the APDC deems necessary and appropriate. If the APDC
  14|                                               does not achieve a majority vote that the alteration is compatible with the
  15|                                               standards and the design guidelines, it shall make a negative
  16|                                               determination. The APDC's written determination shall be submitted to
  17|                                              the property owner after within 20 days of the close of the public hearing,
  18|                                               and no later than 75 90 days from after the receipt submittal of an
  19|                                               application, unless granted a time extension by the owner. In When
  20|                                               making its determination, the APDC shall provide a rationale for its action,
  21|                                              including a description of how the alteration does or does not meet the
  22|                                               standards set forth in § 113-9 and the design guidelines. If the APDC
  23|                                               denies a certificate to alter, it may include nonbinding, advisory only
  24|                                              recommendations on how the proposed alterations may be modified to
  25|                                               satisfy the APDC. Once the applicant modifies the alterations in a manner
  26|                                              that the APDC finds acceptable, the APDC shall issue a certificate to alter.
  27|                                               The determinations of the APDC relative to alterations described in this §
  28|                                               113-8, whether positive or negative, shall be binding on the applicant.

  14|             sentences therefor: "The Annual Town Meeting shall be held on the first Saturday in April commencing
  15|             at 9:00 a.m., and all required reports shall be made at that time. The Annual Town Election shall be held
  16|             on the third Saturday in April commencing at 7:00 a.m. and ending at 5:00 p.m."; or take any other

  18|             Recommended. Article 15 was DEFEATED.

  23|                  *After tallying for the night some ballots were inadvertently put in the B1 transport case.
  24|             The 1st Middlesex Representative race, which included Precincts A, C, D and sub precinct B1 had Mr.
  25|             Shepherd ahead in Lunenburg but the district tally had Ms. Scarsdale ahead by 17 votes - this triggered a
  26|             recount. On December 10, 2022 Lunenburg conducted the recount in the Passios Building Cafeteria.
  27|             Townsend Town Clerk, Kathleen Spofford and Pepperell Town Clerk, Brynn Durno, who had already
  28|             completed their own recount for this election showed up and said we're helping! They came in and took over
  29|             the preparation of the precinct blocks. Their expertise and that of Town Counsel, Adam Costa made the entire
  30|             process much less stressful for all. The Registrars and I would like to thank all those who volunteered their
  31|             time on a Saturday to get this job done. The total difference between the two candidates ended up being seven
  32|             votes district wide.
